## THE ANSWER IN ONE PARAGRAPH (for the DM)

Every town big enough to keep a watch puts a few riders on its own roads. How many, and how good, is set by four things the world already knows: the town's size (a hamlet keeps one thin patrol at most, a city many), its people (the same men the levy would call), its wealth (what it can pay them), and how seriously it keeps order. War and fear move the number inside those limits: more riders out when an enemy is believed near or the town is at war, fewer in quiet years; when the town's army goes on campaign its riders keep the army's road out first, and the back roads go to the brigands unless the town keeps riders there too — men it then does not send with the army. Each week the riders walk a round of the town's roads, the most dangerous and the richest first; where they walk, bandits strike less often, smugglers are caught more often, spies and enemy captains risk being stopped, and the town hears what passes on its land. When the town believes something is out there, its riders go and look; when the thing is bigger than one patrol, several ride together; when it is gone, or was never there, they go back to their rounds — so a false report costs a town its watch for a while. When war comes, the riders are the first to see it and the first to die buying the town a week. Allied towns' riders pass each other at the shared gates and trade what they saw. Inside a kingdom every patrol rides under its own town's banner and every banner above it to the crown's: where the crown's hold is strong the kingdom's riders are one net across the members' borders — a town whose riders went to the war is covered by the crown's and its neighbours' — where the hold is weak each lord keeps his own roads, and where a lord rebels the crown's riders are strangers at his border. The riders also HUNT what keeps off the roads — the gangs, the outlawed flocks and the beasts — by what their town believes of where the thing went and the hidden paths it knows, and they leave alone the flocks and bands their court lets be. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7 THE BANNER · BD's routes (the hunt): the r3 sentence "a kingdom whose hold is strong sends the crown's riders down every member's roads" is widened to the owner's banner and the bodies.] Nothing the riders do is a second law: each effect is a bounded lean inside a number the world already keeps. The DM sees the deeds ("Kell's riders came in from the north road with word of Merewick's host at the ford"); the reasons wait for a look on purpose; the DM can direct the riders, hold their pay down (or raise the town's purse to raise it), and decide every clash.

When it lands, winter SLOWS a pass — toward `spatialCost.js :: SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX` ("Every value FINITE and ≥ 1, and ≤ SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX"; `distanceRead.js`: "even a max-winter mountain hop is SLOW, never infinite") — and NEVER closes it (the host document's law 15: "Weather slows travel, never cuts it"). PR inherits that bound through `hopWeeks` and pins it (R26). ### 0.4 Population facts that bound the design
- Territories are the host's seat partition (`spatialDigest.js`: every land cell to its cheapest-reached settlement; the gate between two neighbours "the cheapest crossing"); a settlement has a handful of neighbours, so a town's ROAD HALVES — the half on its own land of `road{seat, n}` for each digest neighbour `n` (§2.2) — number single digits; a whole world's stretch-weeks are bounded (tens of settlements × single-digit stretches).
- Patrols are FEW by the tier ceiling (§3.1): at most twelve per metropolis, one per hamlet, none per thorp.
- No patrol exists anywhere today (`patrol` as a code token: 0 files outside generation prose — `cure/L-PR-probe/tokens.out`).

## §1 THE LAWS (PR1–PR19)

**PR1 — A PATROL IS A DETACHMENT OF ITS SETTLEMENT'S OWN FIGHTING STRENGTH, PLACED WEEKLY, NEVER STEPPED.** A patrol is a small body of the town's garrison and levy that stands, each week, on exactly one PLACE: a road HALF of its round (the half on the town's own land of a road to a neighbour, cut at the digest gate — §2.2), a site, a looked-at place, or home at rest [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-17 · SEAMS-r2-8: "a gate" is not a place kind (`ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS`); a gate KEYS a road half]. ONE PLACE PER RIDER PER WEEK is a law the round's allocation keeps by construction (§2.3: a STANDING rider walks one half every week; a ROVING rider walks one half of its cycle a week, or is home), so the town's present halves in any week never outnumber its riders [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — the verifier's carry (PR-r2-verify, "§2.3 gives `count` riders the `count` weekly halves plus the phase-hit 4- and 13-week halves, against PR1's 'exactly one PLACE'"): the r2b bucketing gave `count` riders up to `3 × count` present halves; EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (3): the round-3 allocation never places a rider twice in 208 weeks over every n 0..12 × H 1..9]. It has no mid-route position and no stepper; a move of more than one hop takes the weeks the light-unit physics prices (`namedPersonLegTicks({ nominalWeeks: hopWeeks(digest, a, b, season) })`), during which it covers nothing. Its identity is positional — `(settlementId, ordinal)` within that week's count — and an ordinary patrol has no record of its own: the week's PLACEMENT is written once per tick as a digest every consumer reads (PR4, §2.7), and what a patrol DOES across weeks (a detachment's errand, a loss, the DM's order) is a row. Macro over micro: the table needs to know where the riders were and what they did, never their marching order. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — ⛔ STOP-PR-0: the brief's "a member of WC's column union (a new unit class `patrol`)" — measured, a `migration.js` column "carries no position" (PS §5.4) and is released at arrival, and WC-11's free unit (`freeUnits.js`) is ownerless and unbuilt; a patrol is neither. WC's people ledger (WC-6, unbuilt) is the slot where a patrol's men become a block when headcounts are conserved.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — RULINGS-ROUND2b STOP-PR-0: ACCEPTED as written.]

**PR2 — THE COUNT AND THE SIZE LIVE INSIDE AN ENVELOPE FOUR GATES SET; WAR AND BELIEF MOVE THEM ONLY INSIDE IT; THE COUNT IS NEVER AUTHORED.** TIER sets the CEILING, the town's PEOPLE (the people ledger's census count against its tier's reference population) set the POOL, prosperity (and the treasury when lit) sets the PAY, internal security sets the WILL (§3.1, the owner's eighth word). Inside that envelope the war state and the town's belief of what is out there set how many ride this week: fewer in peace (as many as the court's posture keeps out), all of them when the town is at war at home or an enemy is believed near (all it KEPT, while its own army is out), and with the town's army ON CAMPAIGN never fewer than in peace and MORE wherever the envelope has room — the army's road out watched first, the back roads left unless the court keeps riders there too (§3.2) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-r2-3 · PR-V10]. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-2 · PR-r2-3: the pool now reads population (RULINGS-ROUND2b), and "more during wartime" holds for the case the owner named, an army in a campaign.] The DM moves the inputs (wages, the town's order, war) and directs the riders (`PATROL_ORDER`, §11); the DM never sets a count.

**PR3 — ONE HEAD COUNT, ONE STRENGTH, ONE DRAW AT DEPLOY.** The riders are men of the town: the POOL reads the people ledger's census count (`settlement.population` — `peopleLedger.js :: POOLS` 'census'), the one head count; their fighting worth is part of the town's ONE strength truth, the capacity model (`buildCapacityLookup`'s `offensive`), from which the field army is SEEDED once, at deploy (`seedDeploymentState`'s `start`). So men on the roads are men not in the field: the riders the court KEEPS on its roads while its army campaigns are subtracted from the army's seed AT DEPLOY — `patrolDraw`, the chooser's trade-off at the moment the army is raised (§6.6), taken BEFORE the opener's target loop so the feasibility gates that choose the target judge the army that will actually march [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V1] — and re-read only at a re-deploy; the per-tick `offensive` and `homeDefense` are never touched, and `resolveSiegeVerdict`'s stateful `currentEffectiveStrength` is untouched (riders at home are home: the walls do not lose men who are home). Whatever the town does not keep on its roads goes with the army (ABSORBED), and the roads it stops watching go unpatrolled; a court that keeps more riders keeps its rear safe and marches a smaller army — that is the decision (§9.1(e)). No second head count and no second strength number exist. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-1 (BLOCKING) · PR-r2-2 (BLOCKING), RULINGS-ROUND2b: the compiled draw on the per-tick `offensive` "ONLY while afield" never reached the army (seeded at deploy, "regardless of mode") and always reached the walls (`homeDefense = offensive − armyAway`) — the owner's trade-off inverted; the compiled pool read the 70%-tier manpower facet, so population never bound.]

**PR4 — BY ADDRESS AND WEEK, NEVER A ROLL; ONE PLACEMENT A WEEK.** Whether a town's riders walk a road half in a given week is a PERIODIC CLOCK: the half's cadence sets the PERIOD (`weekly` 1 · `every_four_weeks` 4 · `every_thirteen_weeks` 13 · `never`) and keyed hashes of the half and the power — never of the week — set the PHASE (a roving half's slot in its rider's cycle, ordered by `kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32` of the half and the power, plus that rover's own base offset, §2.3 [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V9 · the carry: `hashInt` is no landed helper, and independent per-half phases could put one rider on two halves in one week]) (the host document's own law: "presence in a week is fixed by a hash of the stretch and the power, so every run agrees"; the map's own phase is likewise fixed "never from the week", HR:813), so `every_four_weeks` means exactly one week in four — the map's meaning of the word. The stage writes the coming week's PLACEMENT once (`spatialLedgers.patrols.placement`, §2.7), and EVERY consumer in that week — the banditry roll, the smuggling roll, the counter term, the slow half, the witness, the encounter, the report, the map's window — reads that one placement (a pin: they agree). Every other draw (notice, contest, look) is a seeded keyed hash with a named stream. Two runs of one world agree on every patrol in every week. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — HOST-r2-6 · PR-r2-15: the compiled presence was a fresh per-week draw (a chance averaging one week in four — a second meaning of the map's cadence word), and nothing about a week's placement was stored, so consumers at different points of one tick could re-derive different placements.]

**PR5 — ONE DANGER, ONE COUNTERFORCE, AND NO GATE ROW.** Patrols change ONLY these quantities the world already keeps, each by ONE bounded lean at ONE named seam (§6, §7): (a) a SUBTRACTION on the ONE presence term of the danger ledger, inside the one suppressor slot of MN §3.3's composition — THE SHARED PRESENCE COMPOSER, a shared leaf-first car (RULINGS-ROUND3 A.3) — (ST's sites and PR's riders, one shared cap), floored at zero, never on the walls term — so a patrol lowers only what raiding menaces and untied dens ADDED to the country's roads — never the walls term and never an enemy column (MN §3.3) — and "peacetime roads carry no danger to relieve" (the host document) holds by construction; (b) a bounded DEVIATION term on the raider row's counterforce, and a coverage term on the criminal corridor's, riding ST's ONE additive post-blend slot under its ONE clamp — reaching a raiding menace's wear only through the stamped counterforce score (MN §5.5 (2)); (c) the banditry ROLL for goods on a watched hop (PR6); (d) the smuggling success chance at the worst gate (§6.4); (e) the field army's SEED at deploy, by the riders kept (`patrolDraw`, PR3) — never the per-tick `offensive` or `homeDefense`; (f) the commons' ONE grievance home, by ONE registered input (over-patrolling, §7.5); (g) the WITNESS read PS consumes (PR10); (h) PR's own WILL gate, by a magistrate's step (§10.5). The hearings and receipts of §9–§11 decide between parties and change nothing else. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-28: "three numbers … and no other" was false against §6–§7; the full list is stated and §7.2's second grievance input is struck · PR-r2-7: MN's slot (2) is filled through the stamp, never added · SEAMS-r2-7: the composition is MN §3.3's one sentence, cited and not restated.] PR NEVER mints a stressor birth gate row: the landed rows "Standing defenses patrol the approaches." and "A practised watch chokes smuggling at the gates." already ARE the standing watch, keyed on the institution roster; a second row would count the same riders twice. PR never touches the ramp's own security relief (`rampThreat`'s `W_SEC × security01`, the watch inside the walls).

**PR6 — THE FAST HALF LOWERS THE ROLL, PER HOP, NEVER THE LEVEL OR THE LOSS.** On the week a patrol (or detachment) actually stands on a road half, the INCIDENT ROLL for goods crossing THAT HOP is lowered: a per-hop multiplier on the hop's danger BEFORE the route's mean, feeding only `banditryLoss`'s fire test (§6.3); the loss SIZE still reads the unwatched mean, a sea hop reads no watch (no naval damper), and the embattlement level — the region's state — is never written by a patrol's presence (item 17: two mechanisms, one truth each, never doubled; their JOINT effect is one composition with one floor, §6.3). Named travellers' rolls live in the frozen roads kernel and feel patrols only through the slow half — RULED (STOP-PR-1): the slow half alone for missions and sea piracy now; the fast half for missions is a roads leaf-first car recorded as a C7 slot. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-5 (RULINGS-ROUND2b: per hop, never a route-wide MAX) · PR-r2-18 (the joint composition) · RULINGS-ROUND2b STOP-PR-1.]

**PR7 — BELIEF NEVER TRUTH, EXCEPT WHERE THE WORLD ACTS.** A town's riders go where the town BELIEVES the trouble is and report what they SAW: a report is a rumor feed entry with the town as its hop-0 witness (the witness idiom), so it is the town's belief and only travels as rumor travels. Courts PLAN on belief — a traveller's court routes around the roads it believes watched (§8.3). The ENCOUNTER itself is the world acting on the traveller and is decided on truth (the patrols that actually walk the stretch), exactly as the roads gauntlet and ES's catch roll are ("the K3 boundary runs between a court's DECISIONS (belief) and the world's OUTCOMES (truth)", ES §3.3).

**PR8 — ONE ENCOUNTER LAW; WHAT A CATCH IS BELONGS TO EACH KIND'S OWNER.** `patrolEncounter` decides whether a traveller on a stretch MET a patrol (truth, PR4) and whether it was NOTICED (a keyed draw against coverage × quality × the traveller's cover). What happens next is never PR's second law: a spy's road catch is ONE roll — PR's notice IS that roll — whose outcome is ES's (`spy_at_target` with WHERE `road`: `caught · uncaught`, the taint cascade); an envoy is STOPPED only as a road hearing, THE ENVOY ARM of PS's `passage_at_the_border` (`stopped · escorted · turned_back`, PS §6.5), never the army interception's `envoy_intercepted`/`envoy_held`; a hostile paragon through CH's town's-gate words at WHERE `road`; the party through PT's `party_gate` widened to WHERE `road` (STOP-PR-2, RULED); goods through `smuggle.js`'s pipeline; a caravan through WY-6's `caravan_seized`; a military unit (an army, a column, a corps) through PS's witness and ladder; a BODY of THE BODIES (a gang, a conclave, a beast) through `patrol_engages` where its court stance is pursued, a report where it is only watched, nothing where it is let be (§8.1, §8.2, BD §9.3). Each power whose riders stand on the half meets the traveller separately — one encounter per power (§8.1). A traveller ON A HIDDEN WAY — a body, a paragon on CH's trail arm, a smuggler's run, a covert envoy, any mover master's hidden franchise admits — is met ONLY by the trail arm, on the way's junction halves of a power that knows the way (`trailWatchOf`, §8.1) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — CH→PR route 3 (`cure/D-CH-r4.md` §6: "a PARAGON on a trail is met by `trailWatchOf` exactly as a body is") · B.4: one seam for every mover on the one rung]. The same law has a second WATCHER: when two bodies meet, MET and NOTICED are this law's and the card is BD's `bodies_meet` (§8.1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's routes (1)(2): one law, widened to bodies as travellers and as watchers]. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-9 · PR-r2-10 · SEAMS-r2-2 (RULINGS-ROUND2b) · PR-r2-6 · STOP-PR-2 RULED · RULINGS-ROUND2b open question 3.]

**PR9 — REACT, GROUP, DISPERSE, BY BELIEF AND BY EFFECT.** A believed threat pulls riders toward it; a threat believed bigger than one patrol's band draws several into ONE detachment of the summed band; when the threat is gone, beaten, or was never there, the detachment disperses and its riders go back to their rounds. Grouping and dispersal are internal acts (receipts, no hearing). A FALSE REPORT therefore costs a town its watch for as long as it takes to look and find nothing — the effect of belief, priced by the world itself. Off their round the riders also HUNT (§5.5): a body their court has marked `hunted` or `outlawed`, or a beast, is pursued at the place the town BELIEVES it went — its last sighting, the trails the town knows, its habits — until it is caught or the trail goes cold; the round they leave goes unridden meanwhile, and a rider outmatched shadows and calls for others, never engages [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (3) · L-BD 20].

**PR10 — THE WITNESS IS THE PATROLS'.** `witnessedCrossing` (PS8) is owned here: a crossing is witnessed when a patrol of the HOLDER walks the holder's half of the gate's road that week (the placement, PR4) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-6 · SEAMS-r2-8: per power and per half; a power-blind MAX could not say "the holder's"], or, under a STRONG banner, a rider of the kingdom's net stands on the holder's half (§10.3 (d): the kingdom's eyes are one) [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — the owner's "operate in unity to cover the entirety of the kingdom region": a net rider placed on a member's half by right sees for that member; the GATE's catch stays the holder's own riders' (J385, §6.4)], or the crossing's PLACE is the holder's own seat (a harbour landing or a circle arrival — never a land crossing whose path merely runs on through the seat), or the unit is too large to hide, or a held watchtower stands on the stretch (ST17). On a HIDDEN WAY (a corridor on master's hidden rung, keyed by THE BODIES' `trail:<a|b>`; PS8's quiet crossing taken off the road) the place is the WAY and ONE arm applies: witnessed only where the holder KNOWS the way (THE BODIES' one known set `knownTrailsOf`, RULINGS-ROUND5 B.13) and the holder's EYES — its own riders, or a net rider standing on its half by right (`holderEyesOf`, §2.6) — stand on one of the way's JUNCTION halves that week (`trailWatchOf`, §2.6; BD §5.2: "a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO") [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.13 · PR-X3 · B.4: the known set named, the net rider counted by formula as the law says, the junctions the ladder's] — the stretch-rider, seat, size and watchtower arms do not apply to a unit off the road (the way's capacity is the hiding law, PS8). A town with no riders is blind to its roads; the quiet crossing succeeds only where no patrol walks [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — the PS→PR routes of `cure/D-PS-r4.md` §5 (applied in this round so none is left unapplied): the trail arm, and the seat arm re-worded (PS's REFINED 3: read literally on a node-keyed path, "the holder's own seat is on the path" would witness every intermediate land crossing, and P8 / R14 could never pass)].

**PR11 — FRIENDS BY BELIEF; ONE HOME AND A STACK OF BANNERS; THE LINE BETWEEN MEMBERS BLURS BY THE CROWN'S HOLD.** Allied patrols know each other as far as their towns' beliefs go, and a patrol crossing an ALLY's or a stranger's border is a military unit crossing: PS's ladder applies, with a wider tolerance for a patrol's small size (§10.1(c)). Inside a KINGDOM every patrol has ONE home — the town that raises, pays and houses it, which has FIRST CALL on its own roads — and a LOYALTY STACK, the home's vassal chain up to the crown as PL reads it, derived each week and never stored (§10.3 (a)). The crown's share of a member's riders is its ONE share of the member's force and decisions (PL §4.7 F.2: "PL4's `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the member's seat weight toward its IMMEDIATE liege, at every tier — never a second share for riders"), and under a strong hold a member LENDS at least one rider while it keeps the home's minimum (RULINGS-ROUND5 B.8, §10.3 (c)) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.8 · PR-X1: the r4 law read the crown's own `crownReachAt` share, under which no sub-vassal ever lent]. Where the crown's OWN hold reads STRONG (`staunch`, `unbroken`: `polityHoldOf(…, rootId, rootId)`, PL §3.1 (e)) the kingdom is ONE NET: a member's riders ride a fellow member's roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER wherever that member believes the hold strong (PS's jurisdiction read — a `banner` permit, no spend, one card defaulting `granted`) — and the crown's riders and the members' lent riders fill the halves a member leaves unwatched where a need stands, under the per-stretch cap; where it is WEAK (`shaky`, `frayed`) each lord keeps his own roads and a neighbour-lord's riders — the crown's included — cross on the ladder with the ally's size tolerance; across a BROKEN rung (a feud, a break, a contest that suspends the link) recognition is lost and the crown's riders are a foreign unit under PS's rules. Members never cross by the charter, whose passage is tolls only (PL §4.5). The crown's presence wears as PL's instruments wear when it sweeps a member's roads unasked (§7.6) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-20] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7 · SEAMS-r3-6 (RULINGS-ROUND3 §F.1–F.5: the r3 law applied the ally tolerance at EVERY hold, let `frayed` mean "members ignore the crown's riders", and gave the crown's riders precedence over a home on its own roads) · the PL→PR route (`cure/D-PL-r3.md` §5; PR-r3-verify ROUTED-UNAPPLIED): the clause "`crownReachAt(…).share01 > 0`, which shuts out a sub-vassal under a frayed link" is STRUCK — under the crown's own `staunch`+ read PL5's reach into a sub-vassal is at least `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE.marginal × POLITY_PARAMOUNT_REACH.staunch` = 0.02 (PL §15.12, PL l.121), so it could never fire].

**PR12 — FIRST TO SEE, FIRST TO DIE.** When war comes, the riders' report is the town's first belief of it; a detachment may screen an invader on its town's land — whether the host marches on that town or crosses it toward another — for ONE bounded week, applied once at the screen's verdict by THE ONE HOLD HELPER's FREEZE mode (the unit held exactly where the prior tick left it, one week, no refund — PS §4.1, RULINGS-ROUND3 A.2) and, where THE PASSAGE is lit, counted into the march's ONE cumulative hold cap (PS9's Bound 2; dark, Bound 1 alone) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PS-r2-15 (A.2)]; PR hosts the screen's card and PS keeps the home-defence battle; then the riders the court does not keep fold into the levy (§9.1). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-19 · RULINGS-ROUND2b open questions 1 and 4.]

**PR13 — FIRST TO LOOK.** An appropriate stressor or an appropriate authored event in the territory sends ONE patrol to look for a week; the look is a belief read of what is there; the report suggests, never decides, for the DM (§9.2).

**PR14 — THE MAP DRAWS, NEVER INVENTS; H16 STAYS SHUT; THE HOST'S FACTS GO OUT THROUGH THE WINDOW.** While PR is dark the map's patrols are its own picture and its legend is true ("patrols change nothing in the simulation"). When PR lights, the host SENDS its patrol facts — each road half's power, cadence word and THE WEEK'S PRESENCE as it was (the placement, PR4), with state and detachment — out through the WINDOW, each on its own channel (the dated acts in the events window H9; the week just lived as a lens-window row H23 beside it; H13 carries the map's patrol inputs) (the chair's ruling: never "opens H16"); the host ASKS the map to draw presence as sent rather than recompute it — the map program's call, a cross-program ask, never a rule on the map — and the legend changes (§13) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — the PL-r2-verify minor ("like every other host fact" names no channel) and the FOLD-r2-verify minor (the "never recomputes" wording reads as a rule on the map program, against the fold's §3.1.26 ↳ r2b "No FP text supersedes a map derivation"): PR is that wording's source, so PR re-words it as an ask]. H16 — the map's INBOUND door — stays untouched and open as the owner left it; the host never consumes the map's sway-derived coverage. `patrolEffectsEnabled` lights only where the map's patrol lens is absent or has adopted the host's facts and legend (§12), so two patrol pictures and a false legend never stand together. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — HOST-r2-5 (RULINGS-ROUND2b) · HOST-r2-6 · PR-r2-14.]

**PR15 — THE SEA AND THE SEASONS ARE ALREADY IN THE BOOKS; PR ADDS NEITHER.** Rounds are LAND road stretches at landing (the host document: "Patrols are per road stretch; water has none"). A port's piracy is S3, "banditry-by-construction" over the port node's embattlement level — so a town's riders, by lowering the presence term at its port node, lower piracy there with no line of their own. A patrol's move is priced by `hopWeeks(…, season)`, so winter slows it over a pass exactly as it slows a caravan. A harbour's watch is the port's own NAVY (W-NAVY: "One navy per port settlement"; sea battles between navies, blockade as a siege — `navalKernel.js`), never a PR round and never a new slot: PR mints no sea patrol and no sea law; the sea's carriers are M8 (lanes, ports, storm seasons), W-NAVY and seaRoads D-6 (S1–S3). Winter, when the climate band's travel consumer lands (H11's re-version; CHAIR-NOTES 13 as corrected), SLOWS a pass toward `SLOW_NOT_SEVER_MAX` and never closes it — PR inherits exactly that bound (R26). [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — R-14.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-1 · PR-r2-4.]

**PR16 — THE MOUNT RULE.** Zero lines in `pulseKernel.js` (1581), `applyWorldPulse.js` (907), `warTermination.js` (818), `roadsKernel.js` (838) and `armyTransitKernel.js` (798) beyond PS's one shared stage call. THE ONE PATROL STAGE is a leaf third at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` (after MN's and ST's); the screen rides PS's `TRANSIT_LEAVES` registration list (third, PS §4.1's one order); the draw rides the opener in `warDeployment.js :: evaluateWarLayer` (685 eff, +2: the drawn capacity taken right after `fromCap`, read by both feasibility gates, the `CONQUEST_MARGIN` prefilter and the seed; and ONE conditional stamp line beside the seed, the landed `joinLedger` idiom — §6.6) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V1] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W2: the stamp's write line was uncounted — `seedDeploymentState` (`warArmyRecord.js`, 72 eff) is NOT edited, so the stamp is written by its own line in the opener]; the stamp's fail-closed normalizer is ONE call in `worldState.js :: normalizeDeployments` (351 eff, +1, the `casusReasons`/`joinLedger` idiom; §16.1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V7]; the riders' declared hidden token `patrol` joins `routeNetworkConsumers.js :: HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`' one frozen array (+0 lines; PR-2's unit, OD-PR-51) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.4]; every lean is a leaf its host calls in ≤ 3 lines; hosts near the ceiling (`stressorDynamics.js` 759) take their lines only through ST's one slot leaf.

**PR17 — THE DM'S HAND IS TOTAL.** `PATROL_ORDER` directs the riders — `first` on `round` puts every rider the envelope allows out, `avoid` on `round` holds the count at the minimum (§11.1) [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — §3.2 said "the DM's order may lift `fielded` to `max` or hold it at the minimum" and no dial said how] — and can hold their pay down (the DM's wages band is a CEILING over the purse's band, §3.1 — RULINGS-ROUND2b ST-r2-1's one band law); every clash and join is a hearing the DM may leave, tilt or decide (requirement 24), and the crown's call on a member's riders is PL's `call_in_answer`, heard there; every row is undone by the advance's undo; the count is never authored (PR2). Surprise is safe because the DM can undo any advance, edit any state and decide any hearing (fold requirement 16's bracket).

**PR18 — BEHIND THE SCENES; THE EVIDENCE LAW.** The SURFACE shows the deed ("Kell's riders confiscated Varn cloth run past the embargo" — the landed branch an embargo gate takes is `confiscation`, PR-r2-23); SCRUTINY shows the stretch, the cadence word, the band, the order, the reasons; the MACHINERY (hashes, multipliers, caps) is never pushed. No sentence forecasts ("the riders will catch …"); a patrol's piece on the map says where it was this week, never where it is going (WY §3b: "A marker is a presence, never an intention"). Patrol lines are chronicle unless a clash, a catch of a named traveller, or a war's first report follows (CHAIR-NOTES 2's push budget).

**PR19 — PURPOSE IS THE DM'S; THE PARTY IS NEVER THE ENGINE'S TARGET.** Riders keep the roads; they never seek the party of their own accord — they ride for it only when a court's DM-visible `party_ambush` laid on a road (PT's kind, whose agent is "the WATCH") sends them: with PR lit that WATCH IS a detachment of the court's riders (aim `pursue`, drawn from the round, its cost borne on the round), and the hearing stays PT's; a `party_warrant` stands at the court's town and is served there, never on a road (PT's card) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — RULINGS-ROUND3 B (PT's row): "'a warrant served on a road' → struck (a warrant stands at a settlement)"; PR's two road citations of the warrant follow]. A stop of the party on the road is the world's ordinary act on a traveller — PT's `party_gate` at WHERE `road` — its default "nothing has happened to the party yet" (requirement 24's party clause). The world hosts the campaign and never competes with it. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-13: PT's WATCH and PR's riders were two road forces for one town, incoherent on scrutiny · STOP-PR-2 RULED.]

---

## §2 THE ONTOLOGY

Every vocabulary below is closed; every token was searched on master (`cure/L-PR-probe/tokens.out`, `tokens2.out`, `literals.out`) and against the fold §5; homonyms are registered in §15.2.

### 2.1 The patrol (derived each week; never stored)
`patrolsOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → Patrol[]`, codepoint-stable, each `{ settlementId, ordinal, command, band, state, place, causedBy }`:
- `command ∈ PATROL_COMMANDS = own · paramount` — the town's own riders, or the crown's riders (§10.3) quartered from the polity's root (PL's class word `paramount`, PL §2); a member's rider LENT to the banner's net keeps `own` — its home's — on a fellow member's half (the half's holder differs, which IS the banner; no third command word) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7]. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — R-8: the brief's "road wardens"; `warden` is a landed role label (`roleCategory.js`), CH's display noun and EC's `route_wardenship` / the map's "wardens' terms" — "the crown's riders" in prose, `paramount` in code.]
- `band ∈` MN's might words `thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable` — the `STRENGTH_BANDS` keys MN mints (SMN-28; the J-CNC-5 keys; floors 78/60/42/26/0 on the 0..100 latent scale) — ONE strength scale for a patrol, a detachment, a menace and a body (THE BODIES' might reads the same keys, BD §2.6) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.3: "an untied band" is THE BODIES' body], so every contest compares like with like. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — the brief's "the army's five words": the army's own landed strength phrases are a display ladder (`ARMY_STRENGTH_PHRASES`); the five keys MN mints are the comparable scale.]
- `state ∈ PATROL_STATES = on_round · converging · engaged · escorting · looking · resting` (§2.4).
- `place` — the ONE WHERE convention (fold §3.4 companion table): `{ kind: 'road', ids: [a, b], half: holderId }` (a road HALF — the road is `road{a, b}`, unordered, keyed `stretch:<a|b>` with the digest's own pair key; the half is the part on `holderId`'s land, cut at the digest gate, §2.2) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-8] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — A.14: the minted `a␟b` → the landed `pairKey` form], `{ kind: 'settlement', ids: [seat] }` (home, resting), `{ kind: 'site', id }` (ST), `{ kind: 'country', ids: [a] }` (a look in the land around a town).

### 2.2 The round
**THE ONE STRETCH KEY** [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-8: `gateKey` was a parameter name only and the stretch key was ambiguous (ordered keys blinded the holder's witness; unordered keys made every round cross a border weekly); the fold's WHERE companion adopts this key at P4 — the chair's]. A ROAD is unordered, `road{a, b}`, keyed `stretch:<a|b>` — the digest's own codepoint-sorted pair key (`spatial/distanceRead.js :: pairKey`: "The codepoint-stable unordered pair key (matches the digest's routeReceipts keys)", "`` a < b ? `${a}|${b}` : `${b}|${a}` ``"; the form `roads/seaRoads.js :: classifyLegModes` uses too) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — A.14 · PR-V9: the minted `a␟b` was a second spelling of the landed key]; a power's HALF of it is the part on its own land, cut at the digest gate between the two territories (`spatialDigest.js`: the gate between two neighbours, "the cheapest crossing"), named by the pair `{ road: stretchKey, holder }` — never a third key form (`roadKey` everywhere in this volume IS this stretch key); `gateKey(prev, B) = road{prev, B}` — the gate is that road's cut point, and B's half is the one a unit entering B's land walks. A named traveller's half is chosen from its LEG's own position (`worldPulse/namedPersonTransit.js :: namedPersonPathPosition`: the leg's `progress01` is LEG-wide — "`nodeIndex = … Math.floor(alongStoredPath * last)`" — and `hopIndex` follows from it): it stands on the origin's half of hop `hopIndex` while `progress01 < (hopIndex + cut01) / last` — the gate's own fraction ALONG THE LEG — and on the destination's half after it [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V8: the r2b text compared the leg's `progress01` with a HOP's `cut01`; EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (4): the leg-fraction test and the hop-local test agree at every tick of a four-node leg]. `cut01` is 0.5 (DRAFT) until the digest carries a gate fraction — the digest's gates today are `{ between, cellA, cellB, cost }`, no fraction (a map-side slot the fold's INBOX records, A.14; cells are optional placement data, never asserted — HOST-r2-17's rule). A sea hop (`classifyLegModes(digest, path)[i] === 'sea'`) has no half: water carries no watch (PR15).

`roundOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → [{ half: { road: [a, b], holder: seat }, priority, cadence }]` — the town's ROAD HALVES: its own half of `road{seat, n}` for every digest neighbour `n` whose territory meets the seat's; aspatially the relationship-graph edges at the seat (one half each). A round never leaves the town's land, so a round never crosses a border (only a pursuit or an escort beyond the gate does — §10.1(c)). ORDERED by a fixed-order priority score (DRAFT weights, UNMEASURED): THE ARMY'S ROAD while the town's army is on campaign — the halves of the deployment's path out of the seat through the town's own land, its supply road, first of all (§3.2) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-3: the brief's "an army on campaign needs patrols on its supply roads most" had no priority term] · believed danger on the half (the town's belief of dens, menaces, bands and hostile units on it — §5.1) · its trade value (the caravan traffic the road carries: `commodityFlow`'s channel strength across it, the entrepôt toll it pays) · recent incidents (the town's in-horizon `patrol_report` receipts and banditry losses on it — and, where HB is lit (`habitConditioningEnabled`), weighted by the town's LEARNED HABIT of riding that stretch: HB's ONE curve on the settlement actor, credited when a report finds a `clash`, a `catch` or a `threat_seen` there (`habit/habitCurve.js :: applyCredit`, "`const step = learnRateFor(lawWord) * severityWeightOf(outcomeRung);`"), read through `habitFactor` ("An ABSENT stock returns EXACTLY the number one") as ONE bounded multiplier on the incident term, under HB's LANDED circumstance class `route_menaced` with the stretch key as its action token — no thirteenth class (`habitVocabulary.js :: HABIT_CLASS_CEILING = 12`: "A thirteenth class is an owner gate, not an edit"), a town's single-digit halves inside `habitCurve.js :: HABIT_TUNING.HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP` ("`HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP: 24,`"); PLAUSIBLE until HB-0's car-0 admits a stretch-keyed token, and dark the incident term is the in-horizon count alone) [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — L-BD 39's "the patrol's round biases toward incident stretches on the curve — PR's round, HB's weight": "a town raided from the hills learns to ride the hill road", with HB's one law and no second learner] · the WAYS the town KNOWS — ONE read, called by name: THE BODIES' `knownTrailsOf(worldState, snapshot, seat, tick)` (BD §2.3: "the town's STORED learned set above ∪ its OFFICE'S WAYS"; RULINGS-ROUND5 B.13) — each known way's JUNCTION halves in the town's land, the road stretches `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest)` names as the way's `junctions` (BD §5.2: "a corridor beside `a|b` meets its own stretch, a bypass meets the stretches at its two ends, and once the map names a junction its key is `junction:<id>` beside `stretch:<a|b>` under the card's WHERE `road (a, b)`"), gain the KNOWN-WAY term — the choke point ("The BELIEVED junctions (the ways a town KNOWS, §2.3) are the patrols' choke points: PR's round plans by them"); a way the OFFICE knows (`how: 'by_office'` — "known AND watched by the office's trade") puts its junction halves FIRST, as a fort's attachment does; a way known only to a node (`upTo`) counts the junctions its known prefix reaches. The round READS NO `watched` mark: BD's stored `watched` is the FACT the round leaves — the stage marks a stored known way `watched` through BD's one writer `bodies/hiddenWays.js` the week its riders stand on one of the way's junction halves (that week's `lived` row) and BD's writer drops the mark after a season with none (BD §2.3; BD's round-5 route to PR (d)) — so the mark records the watch and never feeds the priority that placed it (no loop) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (4)] [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.13 · BD-r4-23 · B.4 · BD-r4-10 · BD's round-5 route (d): the r4 term named BD's stored set alone, one junction per way, and made a `watched` mark that PR's own placement writes a first-priority input of that same placement] · a fort's attachment (ST §7.4's coverage row for PR — a held `fortification` at hand puts its halves FIRST on its holder's round; the riders who walk them are the holder's own, and the keep's GARRISON is the site's own complement whose road rows stand beside theirs, §10.5, RULINGS-ROUND3 A.1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — A.1: the r2b "walls without riders are walls" denied the keep its own garrison's road rows] · the DM's order (§11.1). The round's ORDER is derived each week; the week's resulting placement is written once (§2.7 `placement`). [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — R-3: the brief's "beat" is the estate's NEWS BEAT (CHAIR-NOTES 2 "a bounded handful of beats"; WC §7.C "The beat inventory"); the patrol's route is its ROUND, the state `on_round`.]

**THE HIDDEN WAYS ON THE ONE LADDER — the words and the one read.** A hidden WAY is a corridor on master's route ladder at its lowest rung — `routeNetworkLedger.js :: ROUTE_GRADES` "`['highway', 'road', 'track', 'hidden']`"; in the map contract's words the rungs `wild · way · trail · track · road · highway`, the host's `hidden` the CLASS {`wild`, `way`} (`refs/MAP-REPLY-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`; RULINGS-ROUND4 §E) — read through ONE read, THE BODIES' `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest) → [{ trailKey, a, b, cover01, capacity, rung, weeksMult, junctions }]` ("ONE read over ONE ladder (B.4) … never a second network or price", BD §2.5), never a graph of PR's. `trail:<a|b>` is BD's corridor KEY ("a key, never a rung word", BD §2.3); PR's `trailKey`, `trailWatchOf` and "the trail arm" name that key and nothing else; the RUNG a way stands at is the route ledger's fact, never a PR word; DM-facing prose says "way" or "path" (the map's legend says the rung) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — SEAMS-r4-16 · BD-r4-24 (the rung words: the map contract's late class) · B.4].

### 2.3 The cadence (per road half, per power) — the map's own four words, in the map's own meaning
`PATROL_CADENCES = weekly · every_four_weeks · every_thirteen_weeks · never` — exactly the host document's definition ("A *cadence* is how often a power's patrol passes a road stretch: weekly, every four weeks, every thirteen weeks, or never", V13 §3.9 (14)) and now its MEANING too — a PERIOD, never an average chance — so the two programs speak ONE vocabulary in ONE sense where the host's facts meet the map's lens in the window (§13). A half's cadence is DERIVED from RIDER-TIME — the `n` riders on the round this week (fielded minus those in detachments, §4.1) over the round's `H` halves in priority order — so that each rider stands on ONE place a week (PR1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — the verifier's carry: the r2b bucketing ("the first `count` halves … `weekly`; the next `count` … `every_four_weeks`; the next `every_thirteen_weeks`") made `count` riders present on up to three times `count` halves in one week]:
- `n = 0` — every half `never`.
- `n ≥ H` — every half `weekly`; the `n − H` spare riders double the round's first halves in order (two riders on one half read their summed band in `watch01`; overlap past that is the reserve).
- `n < H` — `r = min(n − 1, ⌈(H − n) / 3⌉)` riders ROVE and the other `s = n − r` STAND, so the round's FIRST half — the army's road on campaign, else the most dangerous or the richest (§2.2) — always has a standing rider: the first `s` halves are `weekly`, one standing rider each (a one-rider town walks its first half only); the remaining `H − s` halves are split, in priority order, into `r` consecutive blocks of near-equal size (earlier blocks the larger), one block per rover, and every roving half takes the rover period `p = 4` (`every_four_weeks`) when `H − s ≤ 4r`, else `p = 13` (`every_thirteen_weeks`), a block never holding more than `p` halves (any half beyond every block is `never`; with a town's single-digit halves, §0.4, a half is `never` only in a town with no rider on the round or one rider — EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (3): over `H` 1..9, `n = 1` leaves 36 of 45 halves `never`, `n ≥ 2` none). A rover walks ONE half of its block a week: its block's halves take SLOTS `0…k−1` ordered by `fnv1a32('patrol:slot:' + stretchKey + ':' + holder + ':' + power)` (codepoint tie-break), the rover's BASE is `fnv1a32('patrol:rover:' + power + ':' + blockIndex) mod p`, and in a week whose slot `(tick + base) mod p` holds no half (`k < p`) the rover is home (PR1's "home at rest"). DRAFT rule, UNMEASURED; PR-5 measures it on the soak corpus.
Presence in a given week (PR4): `onStretchThisWeek(half, power, tick)` = `true` for a `weekly` half; for a roving half `(tick + base) mod p === slot` (stored as `phase = (slot − base) mod p`, so presence is `tick mod p === phase`, §2.7); `false` for `never` — the week never enters a hash (`kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32`, the zero-draw key hash the decision module `treatySuccessionDecision.js` already imports; `hashInt` is no landed helper — PR-V9). EXECUTED (`arith.out` (3), every `n` 0..12 × `H` 1..9 over 208 weeks): 0 weeks with more rider-places than riders, and every half present EXACTLY its word's period (`weekly` 208/208 · `every_four_weeks` 52/208 · `every_thirteen_weeks` 16/208) — the map's meaning of the words, kept.
THE CLOCK IS LEARNABLE, AND THAT IS ITS PRICE (L-BD 39 (b), the owner's "the patrol in their pursuit and evasion"). Presence is periodic and fixed by address (PR4; the host document's law "Patrols by address and week, never a roll"), so a watcher that has seen the riders on a half twice can name its period and phase: every court's `believedWatch` (§8.3) and a body's own habit record (THE BODIES, on HB's body grain) read that BELIEVED phase — "The Hollow Road gang has learned Kell's riders come on the fourth week" (scrutiny) — and a body plans its raid between the visits. PR adds NO seeded variation to the round (a roll would break PR4 and the host's law): the round's VARIATION is the world's own — a change of the count re-allocates at once, a believed threat or a hunt pulls riders off, the dwell re-buckets a reordered round, a lent rider or the crown's riders change a half's watch, and the DM's `PATROL_ORDER` breaks a rut (§11.1); a habitual round is caught out, which is the habit law's price ("being known is the price", HB) [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — L-BD 39: the patrol's round as a habit a body can learn, with no second learning law and no roll in the round].
THE DWELL damps re-bucketing at a CONSTANT count: when the round's priority reorders but `n` is unchanged, the newly derived allocation is held as a CANDIDATE (each changed half's `pendingCadence` and `pendingSince` in the placement digest, §2.7 [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V5: the r2b shape `{ cadence, since, on }` had no candidate for the dwell to time]) and the allocation switches WHOLE — every changed half in the same week — once the candidate has held `CADENCE_DWELL` weeks unchanged (DRAFT 4, UNMEASURED), so a half-switched allocation can never put one rider in two places; a FIRST-RESPONSE change (war at home, a believed threat on a half, the army's road, the DM's `first`) or a CHANGE OF THE COUNT (up by first response; down by a levy, a loss, the pay or the will) re-allocates AT ONCE (the candidate is cleared), so the dwell never slows a war and no cadence word ever promises a rider the town does not field. The dwell keeps quiet years quiet and never slows a war. The host ASKS the map to draw its PRESENCE as sent rather than recompute it from the cadence word (the map's own phase is keyed from the road segment's lineage, so two clocks would part — HOST-r2-6); the map program decides (§13). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — HOST-r2-6 · PR-r2-14 (the dwell).] [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — R-2: the brief's count band `none · thin · fair · strong` is not minted — `thin` and `strong` are MN's might words on the same cards, `thin` is also `RESERVE_BANDS`' and `fair` PS's tolerance band; the count is an integer (never stored) and a stretch's coverage speaks the map's cadence words.]

### 2.4 The states
| State | What the patrol is doing this week | Covers its stretch? |
|---|---|---|
| `on_round` | walking the road half its round's clock placed it on (the placement, PR4) | yes |
| `converging` | riding toward a believed threat or a muster point (a move of more than one hop) | no |
| `engaged` | in a detachment at the threat: clearing, screening, pursuing | the stretch it stands on |
| `escorting` | riding beside a caravan, a column under PS's watch, or an envoy of its town, through its own land | the stretches it rides |
| `looking` | checking out a stressor or an authored event (§9.2), one week | the place looked at only |
| `resting` | at home after a detachment's errand ran past the rest bound (§7.3) | no |

"Dispersed" is the EVENT that returns a detachment's riders to `on_round` (a receipt), never a state. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — the brief's six states had `dispersed` as a state; it is an event (and `dispersed` is a landed lifecycle stamp, `settlementLifecycleFirstClass.js`); `looking` added for the owner's fourth word.]

### 2.5 The detachment
When several riders act together (§5.2) they form ONE detachment: `{ detachmentId, settlementId, members: [ordinal…], allies?: [{ settlementId, members }], band, aim, place, sinceTick, causedBy, pursuedRef?, leaderRef? }` — `aim ∈ DETACHMENT_AIMS = clear · screen · pursue · escort · look` (`look` is a detachment of one). Its band is the members' summed latent strength, banded on the same five words. An allied town's riders — or a fellow member's under the banner (§10.3) — who JOIN (§10.2) keep their own command inside it (two commands on one stretch, WC's mirror-the-leg idiom) and are credited separately. `pursue` serves TWO quarries, never more: PT's road WATCH (a `party_ambush`, PR19 — its target is the card's, never stored here) and a BODY (THE HUNT, §5.5) — whose ref is `pursuedRef: { kind: 'body', id }` (the sixth actor kind, RULINGS-ROUND4 A.12) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (3): `pursue` widened from the party's watch to bodies]. `leaderRef: { kind: 'person', id: <wnpcId> }` names a HUNT'S LEADER where a mission is taken (L-BD 40, §5.5) — a REFERENCE only: PR never moves a person's record, and no verdict on the detachment ever touches the leader (no named fate) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — L-BD 40: "the detachment under a named leader"]. It is the leader THE MISSION'S CARD names (BD's `hunt_taken`, §5.5) — the hunters' leader, never a BODY's: a body's leader has ONE record, CH's `paragon.leads`, which BD reads (`leaderOf`) and nobody copies (RULINGS-ROUND5 B.12) [REFINED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.12's one leader record kept apart from the hunt's reference: two units, two leaders, one word in one sense]. Both fields are drop-when-absent additions declared under OD-PR-45 (the owner's shape, visible). Tokens: `pursuedRef` and `leaderRef` FREE in `src` and `tests` (`cure/D-PR-r4-probe/tokens-r4.out`; `leaderRef` has one prose hit in a ledger doc, another surface); `quarry` (34 src files, 8 quoted literals — the stone quarry) and `leader` (42 src files) AVOIDED as bare names.

### 2.6 The derived reads (signatures; all pure; all in leaves under `src/domain/patrols/`)
- `patrolEnvelopeOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId) → { ceiling, pool, pay, will, max, fielded, band, reasons[] }` (§3).
- `patrolsOf(…)`, `roundOf(…)` (above); `onStretchThisWeek(half, power, tick)` (PR4, the periodic clock over the rider-time allocation, §2.3); `patrolPlacementOf(worldState, settlementId, tick)` — the week's ONE placement every consumer reads (the digest row whose `forTick` is `tick`, §2.7); no consumer derives a placement of its own [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-15].
- `coverageOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, tick) → coverage01` — THE SLOW SIGNAL: the mean of the town's last four weekly coverages, read from the placement digest's ring (each week's share of its round walked, weighted by priority, × quality — band, pay, fatigue) (§6.1, §6.2) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-25: "this week" and "over the last four weeks" were two definitions, and past weeks were not stored; the ring is the one].
- `watchOn(worldState, snapshot, roadKey, tick) → watch01` — THE DANGER READ the danger leans call: the MAX over every power whose riders stand on either half of the road this week (from the placement) of `quality(band, pay)` — one coverage per road, overlap is waste (item 12(f)) — 0 when none.
- `watchOf(worldState, snapshot, half, tick, power) → watch01` — THE PER-POWER READ, keyed by the HALF (`{ road: stretchKey, holder }`): that power's riders' quality on that half this week, 0 when its riders are not on it. The encounter runs once per power present on the traveller's half (§8.1); the smuggling lean reads the gate town's riders on its own half (§6.4); the witness reads the holder's on the holder's half (§6.5) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-6: `watchOn` returns a number and could not say "the holder's power"] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V8: the r2b read was keyed by the whole road, so MET tested the whole road after choosing a half].
- `holderEyesOf(worldState, snapshot, half, tick) → watch01` — THE HOLDER'S EYES on its own half: the MAX of `watchOf(…, half, tick, half.holder)` (its own riders) and `watchOf(…, half, tick, p)` for every power `p` whose NET row stands on that half this week (§2.7: "the net's rows on a fellow member's half are this power's half rows keyed by that holder" — placed only where the holder answers `right`, §10.3 (d)); 0 when neither. ONE read for the two consumers PR10 names — the witness (§6.5) and the trail arm (`trailWatchOf`, below) — and NEVER the gate's catch (the smuggling lean reads the holder's own riders only: J385, §6.4) nor the encounter (which runs once per power, §8.1) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PR-X3 (minor): PR10 says "a net rider … sees for that member" while both formulas read `watchOf(…, holderId)`, the holder's own riders only].
- `patrolEncounter({ worldState, snapshot, half, traveller, tick, power }) → { met, noticed, by, reasons[] }` — one call per power on the half (§8.1).
- `witnessedCrossing(worldState, holderId, gateKey, unit, tick) → boolean` (PR10; PS8's read; `gateKey = road{prev, B}`, §2.2).
- `believedWatch(worldState, snapshot, observerId, roadKey, tick) → { watch01, known }` — what a court BELIEVES the watch on a road to be, from NAMED producers (§8.3): its own riders' `riders_seen` reports at a shared gate, its allies' exchanged reports, its travellers' return receipts, its spies' product, rumor of a clash or a catch on the road; with nothing heard it returns the TIER-REFERENCE PRIOR of the road's holder (the coverage a funded town of that tier fields at peace) with `known: false`, and the card says "unknown" — the ONE planning read [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-16: no producer was named and `null` was undefined]. Its return is `{ watch01, known, phase? }` — `phase` the BELIEVED period and slot where two or more sightings on the half fix them (§2.3's learnable clock; L-BD 39); a TRAIL key `trail:<a|b>` is read too: `watch01` is the believed watch of the way's junction halves (the stretches `hiddenWayEdges` names as its `junctions`, §2.2) where the observer believes the holder KNOWS the way (it has heard of a catch on it, or of a warden holding the holder's public-security office — §5.5), else 0 with `known: false` — never the holder's true knowledge; and for a BODY observer the read is BANNER-BLIND (the MAX over every power it has heard of on the road, whoever's riders they are — "A BODY sees one banner", RULINGS-ROUND3 F.4) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (7) · L-BD 39 · L-BD 41 (b)].
- `trailWatchOf(worldState, snapshot, power, trailKey, tick) → watch01` — THE ONE COVERAGE READ ON A HIDDEN WAY (BD §5.2): `known = knownTrailsOf(worldState, snapshot, power, tick).get(trailKey)` — THE BODIES' ONE known set, called by name ("PS's `hiddenWayFor`, PR's `trailWatchOf` and CH's routing all call it", RULINGS-ROUND5 B.13); where `known` stands, the MAX over the way's JUNCTION halves in the power's land that its known prefix reaches (the `junctions` of `hiddenWayEdges(worldState, digest)`, §2.2; `known.upTo` bounds a partial knowledge) of `holderEyesOf(…, { road: junction, holder: power }, tick)`, else 0 — the power's EYES (its own riders, or a net rider by right, above) on a junction half this week, the week's ONE placement (R30) being the watch, so the stored `watched` mark is never re-read for coverage (it is the fact this placement writes, §2.2); ZERO on a way the power does not know (BD §5.2: "a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO; on a known WATCHED way it is the round's coverage at the junctions"); the office's ways count whole and watched by the office's trade (BD §2.3 `by_office`) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (4)] [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.13 · BD-r4-23 (the union unnamed) · B.4 · BD-r4-10 (a way may meet several roads; the bypass's two ends) · PR-X3 (the net rider counted by formula)].
- `predictedPlaceOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, bodyId, tick) → { place, confidence, reasons } | null` — THE HUNT'S PREDICTION (§5.5), on BELIEF only: the town's ONE belief record of the body, BD's `believedBodyOf(worldState, townId, bodyId) → { known, mightBand?, baseId?, lastWhereId?, lastWhat?, lastTick?, leaderRef?, source }` (BD §2.4: "DERIVED ON READ from the town's rumor records carrying the body's id … and from the SIGHTINGS registered as rows of the same source" — a site's sighting, a patrol's report, "an INFORMER's word where the commons fear the body", a caught operative's confession), the ways the town knows (`knownTrailsOf`), and the body's habits (HB's body grain, else BD's `BODY_HABITS` as the kind's prior); `null` when the town has nothing to go on [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — BD→PR route (9) (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` §5: "`informer` sightings in the town's belief"; BD §7.6: "PR's `predictedPlaceOf` reads the sighting"): the r4 read was `believedBodies`, the list form, whose return carries no might and no informer row]. One read for every mover that follows the hunt (the detachment; a paragon leader's own mover, CH) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (3) · L-BD 20 · L-BD 40].
- `bannerCoverOf(worldState, memberId, tick) → { weeks, seasonStartTick }` — THE BANNER'S SEASON LEDGER read (§10.3 (f); RULINGS-ROUND5 B.9: "The banner's record carries a per-member seasonal tally `weeksCovered` (reset at the season; a one-field addition, declared) that PL F.4/P49's protection rows read at the season's end — never the one-week `lived` rows"): the member's `weeksCovered` pair (§2.7) — the weeks of the CURRENT season (`seasonStartTick = tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`) in which a rider of a power other than the member's own stood PLACED on at least one of its road halves — `weeks` 0 when the pair is absent or of another season; the fact PL's protection rows and its precedence friction read (PL §4.7 F.4, F.6 (b) (i)) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PR-X2 (MAJOR) · B.9: the r4 read took a span from the placement digest's `lived` rows, which hold ONE week, so a net that rode weeks 1–10 and slipped wrote `protection_failed` at the season's end — EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r5-probe/season-r5.out` (a): r4 `protection_failed`, r5 10 weeks → `protection_kept`]. The LOYALTY STACK is PL's BANNER STACK (PL §2: `polityOf(…, homeId).path` with `crownReachAt` at each rung, CUT at the first `swornLinkSuspended` link — "NO NEW READ"), and whether a crossing is BY RIGHT is PS's `passageStandingOf(…).banner` (`right · tolerance`; PS §5.5) — PR keeps no read of either [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7, aligned with PL §2/§4.7 and PS §5.5].
- `patrolDrawOf(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, keptRiders) → points` and `patrolDrawnCap(worldState, snapshot, settlementId, cap) → { cap, drawShare01, kept, points }` — read ONCE per opener pass, right after `fromCap`, by the opener's feasibility gates, its `CONQUEST_MARGIN` prefilter and the seed (PR3; §6.6) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V1]; `points` is the strength ACTUALLY drawn, `min(patrolDrawOf(…), cap.offensive)`, and `cap` is `fromCap` itself (the same object, byte-identical) when nothing is drawn — nothing kept, or nothing to draw from — so `drawShare01` is 1 there by construction and is taken only over a positive offensive, with its 0/0 guard reading 0 (§6.6) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W3].

### 2.7 The persisted shape — ONE ledger, four keyed maps (`spatialLedgers.patrols`, drop-when-empty)
The ledger nests under `spatialLedgers` (the FP-R namespace: "ZERO eager first-paint bytes", the `armyTransit` precedent) and is ABSENT whenever it would be empty.

| Map | Key | Row (closed fields) | Bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| `detachments` | `detachmentId = fnv([settlementId, aim, placeKey, sinceTick])` | §2.5's fields + `weeksOut` (the optional `pursuedRef` — a body — and `leaderRef` — a person — included [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (3) · L-BD 40]) | at most `count` per town (a rider is in one detachment); dropped at dispersal (the receipt keeps the fact) — a hunt also at its cold trail (§5.5) |
| `losses` | `settlementId` | rows `{ tick, band, causedBy }`, newest first — each a `cut_down` verdict or default, the only way riders are lost (an absorption into the levy writes nothing) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-20: the closed field `cause ('cut_down'·'absorbed')` contradicted §3.2's "written as nothing"] | ≤ 4 rows per town; a row stops counting at its RECOVERY (DRAFT one season, `INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season`) and is dropped at write past two seasons (requirement 23's horizon) |
| `orders` | `settlementId` | `{ place?, priority? ('first'·'avoid'·'clear'), wages? (FUNDING_BANDS member), setTick, outcomeRef }` — the DM's overlay (§11.1) | one per town; cleared by the DM (`priority: 'none'`, `wages: 'derived'`) |
| `placement` | `settlementId` (the riders' power) | `{ next: { forTick, halves: { [stretchKey]: { [holder]: { cadence, since, phase?, on, pendingCadence?, pendingSince? } } } }, lived: { forTick, on: [{ road, holder }…] }, coverage: [c₋₃, c₋₂, c₋₁, c₀], forkSince?, weeksCovered? }` — `weeksCovered` THE BANNER'S SEASON LEDGER for this town AS A MEMBER: the pair `{ seasonStartTick, weeks }`, `weeks` the count of weeks of the season beginning `seasonStartTick` (`tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`, PL's season boundary) in which a rider of ANY OTHER power stood placed on at least one of this town's road halves — counted by the stage at step 5 from every power's `lived` row of the week just lived (an entry `{ road, holder }` whose holder is this town on another power's row: a round never leaves its own land, §2.2, so every such entry is THE NET's), ONE count per week however many riders; the first counted week of a season REPLACES the pair (never summed across seasons); read only through `bannerCoverOf` (§2.6) by PL's protection and friction rows; ⭐ OD-PR-50, the owner's shape, declared [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PR-X2 · RULINGS-ROUND5 B.9: "a one-field addition, declared"]; `forkSince` the first tick of the CURRENT unbroken run of weeks in which the town was at peace with nothing believed near and its peace-posture fork read `peaceShare > PEACE_SHARE` (§3.2), the one carrier of §7.5's "held one season" (written at the run's first week, kept while it holds, deleted the week it breaks) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W1 (RULINGS-ROUND4 B: "`forkSince` DECLARED on the placement row with its lifecycle — the owner's 'held for a season' intent kept; a one-field addition to `spatialLedgers.patrols`, declared as the owner's shape"; OD-PR-44)]; the net's rows on a fellow member's half (§10.3 (d)) are this power's half rows keyed by that holder, `weekly` while their need stands — THE WEEK'S ONE PLACEMENT: the stage at tick t writes `next` for t+1 (each half's cadence word, the tick it took that word, a roving half's `phase` — the residue `tick mod p` it is walked in, stored so the held allocation stays exact through the dwell — whether the riders stand on it that week, and the dwell's CANDIDATE word with the tick it was first derived; the half is keyed by the stretch and its holder, the crown's riders on a member's half included) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V5 (the candidate) · the carry (the phase) · A.14 (the stretch key)] and moves the old `next` to `lived`; every consumer in tick t+1 reads the row whose `forTick` is its tick; the window after the advance reads `lived` (the week just lived — never a week ahead: "a marker is a presence, never an intention"); `coverage` is the four-week ring, one number a week [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-15 · PR-r2-25 · PR-r2-14 · HOST-r2-6] | one row per town fielding riders — or carrying a season tally (a member whose own riders went to the levy keeps the row for its `weeksCovered` alone, `next`/`lived`/`coverage` then absent) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.9]; a town fielding none and carrying no tally has no row (drop-when-empty); the ring holds four numbers, never more; the tally one pair, one season; machinery — never shown ahead of its week |

**Persisted shapes: ONE new (four maps of one ledger)** — with THREE drop-when-absent fields added in round 4 (`forkSince` on `placement`; `pursuedRef` and `leaderRef` on `detachments`), each DECLARED AS THE OWNER'S SHAPE and visible for his veto (OD-PR-44 · OD-PR-45; their lifecycles §16.1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W1 · BD's route (3) · L-BD 40], and ONE in round 5 (`weeksCovered` on `placement`, ⭐ OD-PR-50, the owner's shape, declared; its lifecycle §16.1) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — B.9]. **Rows added to landed shapes (no new shape):** ONE conditional stamp on the landed deployment record — `patrolDraw: { riders, points }`, written by ONE conditional line in the opener beside the seed WHENEVER THE COURT KEEPS RIDERS (`if (drawn.kept > 0) seededRecord.patrolDraw = { riders: drawn.kept, points: drawn.points };` — the landed idiom of the line after it, "`if (coalitionDecision) seededRecord.joinLedger = [coalitionDecision.anchor];`"; `seedDeploymentState`, `warArmyRecord.js` 72 eff, is not edited) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W2 · PR-W3: the line is counted (PR16, §14 PR-1), and `points` is the strength actually drawn, never more than the offensive it came from] [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PR-X5 (minor): the r4 line stamped only when `points > 0`, so an army with no offensive to draw from that kept riders carried no `riders` and "while that army is out `fielded` never exceeds them" had no carrier; the stamp is now written whenever riders are kept, `points` 0 where nothing could be drawn] (the W-C1 receipts' "stamp ONLY when non-trivial" idiom — kept riders ARE the non-trivial fact; absent ⇒ the record is byte-identical, as it is dark and for a court that keeps none), read by the stage to bound the riders fielded while that army is out (§3.2) and by the siege's Why line [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-1], validated fail-closed by its own block in the deployment normalizer (§16.1) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V7]; (a stretch key), power?, band?, subjectRef? }`, `finding ∈ threat_seen · crossing_seen · riders_seen · found_nothing · catch · clash`; the rumor ledgers' own lifecycle) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-16]; PS's crossing rows written with `witnessed` from PR's read (PS's shape); ONE `MOVEMENT_SITES` row (a test manifest); the registry rows of §11.2 (SP-9's ledger); the realm-manifest row of `PATROL_ORDER`.

## §3 THE ENVELOPE — HOW MANY, AND HOW GOOD (the owner's first and eighth words; items 2 and 19)

### 3.1 The four gates (each an existing read, named; every number DRAFT, UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster)

| Gate | The read | What it sets |
|---|---|---|
| TIER → the CEILING | `settlement.tier` on `SIZE_LADDER` | the most patrols and the best single band the town can ever field, whatever its wages: `thorp` 0 · `hamlet` 1 (`thin`) · `village` 2 (`thin`) · `town` 4 (`modest`) · `city` 8 (`modest`) · `metropolis` 12 (`capable`) — a metropolis keeps a standing watch whose detachments reach `strong` or `formidable` only by grouping |
| POPULATION → the POOL | the people ledger's ACTUAL census count, `settlement.population` (`peopleLedger.js :: POOLS` 'census'), against its tier's REFERENCE population — the midpoint of the tier's `POPULATION_RANGES` band, `(min + max) / 2` | `pool = max(min(1, ceiling), round(ceiling × clamp01(population / tierMidpoint)))` — a SMALL TOWN OF ITS TIER fields fewer than a full one (EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r2-probe/pool-r2.out`: binds in 7 of 24 tier×population rows — a 901-soul town 1 of 4, a 5,001-soul city 3 of 8, a 25,001-soul metropolis 5 of 12; a hamlet keeps its one) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-2 (BLOCKING), RULINGS-ROUND2b: the compiled `facets.manpower` read is 70% tier, so the pool equalled the ceiling in every case; the manpower facet is NOT the read] |
| PROSPERITY → the PAY | THE BAND, always on: the settlement's PROSPERITY-DERIVED band from the landed ladder, through ST's ONE band law exported SETTLEMENT-keyed (`settlementFundingBand`, ST §2.4 — THE ONE settlement band, which ST mints and names [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V6 · the ST-r2-verify note 2 (A.9): PR's `fundingBandOfSettlement` was a second name for one read], ST's settlement arm — `prosperityRank01 < 0.2` `unfunded` · `< 0.45` `meagre` · `< 0.8` `funded` · else `lavish`, ST §2.4; ST exports it, done in ST's round 2b); the DM's `wages` overlay is a CEILING over it — EFFECTIVE = min(the DM's band, the purse's band) (RULINGS-ROUND2b ST-r2-1: one band law for sites and riders), so a town whose prosperity falls lets its riders thin before W-COIN lights. THE COIN is separate and a slot: when W-COIN lights, the treasury's `UPKEEP_LEDGERS` gain a `patrols` row beside `sites` and `retainers` (RULINGS-ROUND2 D; the W-COIN inbox; its leaf-first car named there) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-12: the owner's eighth word ("gated by … prosperity") stays live whatever the coin's state; ST's read was SITE-keyed] | `PAY_SHARE`: `unfunded` 0.25 · `meagre` 0.5 · `funded` 0.85 · `lavish` 1.0 of the pool; and the BAND: `unfunded` one band below the ceiling's, `lavish` the ceiling's |
| INTERNAL SECURITY → the WILL | `defenseLedger(settlement).internal` (the town's security investment; 0..100), floored by the live `security01Of` (a lawless town cannot claim its generated order); ONE named lean steps it — a magistrate's order (CH, §10.5), one step, never past 1.0 [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-r2-8 (A.1): the r2b `fortWill` (a sound keep raising the holder's will one step, "the fort's road arm when PR is lit") is STRUCK — it existed only because the fort's own road rows were made to read 0 under the patrols; A.1 restores those rows lit or dark, so a will step from the same keep would count the keep twice on the roads. The owner's "increase the internal secruity of said related terriotry" is ST's own rows (×0.9 "The keep's garrison keeps order on the roads.", ST §3.3), never PR's] | `WILL_SHARE`: `internal < 30` → the minimum (one patrol where the ceiling allows one, never more) · `< 60` 0.7 · `< 80` 0.9 · else 1.0 |

`max = clamp(floor(min(pool, ceiling) × PAY_SHARE × WILL_SHARE), min(1, ceiling), ceiling)` minus every `losses` row still within its recovery; the BAND is the ceiling's band stepped down one for `unfunded` pay or a will below 30, never below `thin` (the floor) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-26: stepping `thin` down was undefined]. Each gate reads its own one truth; none is a second number, and none is stored. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final) — the `thorp` row is not in the owner's list: the size ladder begins at `thorp` (`tierFacts.js`), a thorp keeps no watch; the "security band" of the brief is NOT `warSeatBooks.js`'s `securityBand` (`secure · holding · precarious`, the RULER's seat security — a registered homonym), it is `defenseLedger`'s `internal`.]

### 3.2 War and belief move the count INSIDE the envelope (the owner's first word)
- **PEACE** — the town's courts at peace and nothing believed near: `fielded = ceil(max × peaceShare)`, `peaceShare = clamp(PEACE_SHARE + PEACE_POSTURE_W × (0.5 − appetite01) + PEACE_ALIGN_W × harsh01, 0.4, 1.0)` — `PEACE_SHARE` 0.6 ("less during peacetime"), the court's own risk appetite (`strategicPosture.js :: courtRiskAppetiteOf`: a wary court keeps more riders out, a bold or lax one fewer; `PEACE_POSTURE_W` 0.4) and the seat's alignment (`settlementAlignment`: a lawful-cruel seat leans to the watch, `harsh01` = lawfulness × malice; `PEACE_ALIGN_W` 0.2) — all DRAFT, UNMEASURED; where the posture layer is dark, the flat 0.6. This fork is what keeps every rider out in quiet years, and it is what §7.5's harassment prices [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-27: no court fork existed, so the counterforce punished only the DM's own order]; the stage records the first week of each unbroken run of such weeks in which the fork reads above `PEACE_SHARE` (`forkSince`, §2.7), so "held one season" is a stored fact [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PR-W1].
- **A BELIEVED THREAT** in the territory or on a half of the round (§5.1) — `fielded` rises toward `max` by the threat's believed band (one step per band above `thin`, DRAFT).
- **AT WAR AT HOME** — the town in a live war front with its army at home, or an enemy army believed in its land: `fielded = max` for the first-response weeks (§9.1); while the town's OWN army is out, an enemy believed in the land raises `fielded` to the KEPT count (what stayed — the stamp's `riders`), never above it: the riders who marched are with the army [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-V10: "an enemy army believed in its land ⇒ `max`" contradicted "while that army is out `fielded` never exceeds" the kept riders].
- **THE ARMY ON CAMPAIGN** (the town's army deployed abroad, `deployments[id].targetId`) — MORE than in peace wherever the envelope has room, and never fewer, as the owner's first word asks ("more during wartime … if they have an army in a campaign"): the riders the court keeps are set ONCE, when the army is raised — by default `kept = max(ceil(max × CAMPAIGN_SHARE), min(max, ceil(max × peaceShare) + 1))`, `CAMPAIGN_SHARE` 0.75 (DRAFT, UNMEASURED) — so the campaign count is NEVER below the court's own peace count and is ONE MORE wherever the envelope has room (equal only where the peace count already stands at `max`) — and up to `max` where the court's chooser keeps more (`keepRiders`, §9.1(e)) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PR-r2-3 (RULINGS-ROUND3 B): the r2b default `ceil(max × 0.75)` fell below a wary or lawful-cruel court's peace count (share 0.80) in 12 of 60 rows; EXECUTED `cure/D-PR-r3-probe/arith.out` (1): below 0 · equal 15 (each with the peace count at `max`) · above 45]; the kept riders are drawn from the army's seed at that deploy (`patrolDraw`, PR3, stamped on the deployment record, §2.7), and while that army is out `fielded` never exceeds them. They ride THE ARMY'S ROAD FIRST — the halves of its path out through the town's own land, its supply road (§2.2's first priority term) — then the rest of the round in priority order; whatever halves they do not reach go UNPATROLLED, and the back roads go to the brigands (felt sentence 9). Everything not kept marched with the army (ABSORBED — written as nothing; the riders are with the army) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PR-r2-3 (BLOCKING): as compiled, an army on campaign held `fielded` at or below the PEACE share even when the court kept its riders, so "more during wartime" could never hold for the case the owner named · PR-r2-1: the draw at deploy].
- **THE TOWN BESIEGED** — the riders are inside the walls: `fielded = 0` on the roads; they are part of `homeDefense` already.
- **THE DM'S ORDER** may lift `fielded` to `max` (`PATROL_ORDER` `first` on `round`) or hold it at the minimum (`avoid` on `round`) (§11.1); never beyond the envelope, and while an army is out never beyond what it left at home.
